\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit B\mathsfit C\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}\end{array}

Figure 4:
Deductive systems for computing stringsums and allsums of CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG.

Deduction rules.
We distinguish several types of pop computations based on their first production and include a deduction rule for each.
The weight of each pop computation can be derived recursively from the weights of shorter pop computations. The weight of each deduction rule is the weight of the production in the side condition.
Notice that the deduction system only shows how to derive further items, leaving the order in which these items should be derived by an algorithm unspecified.

Goal item.
The goal item is
\mathit{S}[\mathsfit{S}]
0
n
, which stands for all derivations from
\mathit{S}[\mathsfit S]
to
\boldsymbol{s}
, where
|\boldsymbol{s}|=n
.
Its weight is the total weight of all such derivations, which is exactly the stringsum.

4.3
Correctness

We prove the correctness of the deduction system for CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG using the following theorem.

Theorem 1
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
be a controller WCFG with nonterminal alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
and start symbol
\mathsfit S
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
a WLD-CFG with nonterminal alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
be a string of length
|\boldsymbol{s}|=n
.

(a)

The weight
w
of an item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
i
j
, where
\mathit{X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
, and
0\leq i\leq j\leq n
, is the total weight of all pop computations of this type.

(b)

The weight of an item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
l
j
k
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
, where
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
,
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
, and
0\leq i\leq j\leq k\leq l\leq n
, is the total weight of all pop computations of this type.

Proof.

Define the
span
of item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
i
j
to be
j-i
, and that of item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
l
j
k
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
to be
j-i+l-k
.
We proceed by induction on the span
\ell
.

Base Case.

When
\ell=1
, the pop computation consists of a single step
{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{a}}
.
The weight of item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
i-1
i
is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p})
according to inference rule (
terminal
).

Inductive Step.

Assume that the statement holds for pop computations with span at most
(\ell-1)
and consider pop computations with span
\ell
.
The weight of all pop computations of a certain type is the sum of various cases, distinguished by their first production.

Starting with
left pop
rules.

Any pop computation starting with
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]
and a
left pop
production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p}
has the form
({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{Z}[\mathsfit S]},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{Z}[\mathsfit S]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(j\mathord{:}k]}})
.

By the inductive hypothesis, the weight
w_{1}
of item
\mathit{Z}[\mathsfit{S}]
j
k
is the total weight of all partial derivations such that
{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{Z}[\mathsfit S]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(j\mathord{:}k]}}
.
By distributivity, the total weight of all pop computations of the above form is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p})\otimes w_{1}
. This weight is added to the weight of the item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
o
k
l
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
in inference rule (
left pop
).

Since the rules are symmetric, a similar argument can be used for pop computations that start with
right pop
rules.

Starting with
push
rules.

Any pop computation starting with
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]
and a
push
production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p}
has the form
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})
, where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1}

\displaystyle=\begin{aligned} {\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit B\mathsfit C]}&\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\mathsfit C]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\\
&\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(j\mathord{:}k]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.3984,0}\mathit{Z}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}},\end{aligned}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2}

\displaystyle={\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(l\mathord{:}m]}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(l\mathord{:}m]}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(m\mathord{:}o]}}.

By the inductive hypothesis, the weight
w_{1}
of item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{B}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
o
j
m
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
is the weight of all pop computations
({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit B]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(m\mathord{:}o]}})
.
Similarly, the weight
w_{2}
of item
\mathit{Y}[\mathsfit{C}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
j
m
k
l
\mathit{Z}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
is the total weight of all pop computations
({\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\mathsfit C]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(j\mathord{:}k]}}{\color[rgb]{1,0.3984,0}\mathit{Z}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}},{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{1}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(l\mathord{:}m]}})
.
By distributivity, the total weight of all such partial derivations is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p})\otimes w_{1}\otimes w_{2}
.
This weight is added to the weight of the item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
o
k
l
\mathit{W}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
in inference rule (
push
-1).

Any pop computation starting with
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]
and a
push
production
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p}
has the form

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit B\mathsfit C]}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\mathsfit C]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(j\mathord{:}k]}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}

\displaystyle\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(j\mathord{:}k]}}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(k\mathord{:}l]}}.

By the inductive hypothesis, the weight
w_{1}
of item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{B}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
l
j
k
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
is the weight of all pop computations
({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit B]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(k\mathord{:}l]}})
, while the weight
w_{2}
of item
\mathit{Y}[\mathsfit{C}]
j
k
is the weight of all pop computations
{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\mathsfit C]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\boldsymbol{s}_{(j\mathord{:}k]}}
.
By distributivity, the total weight of all such partial derivations is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p})\otimes w_{1}\otimes w_{2}
.
This weight is added to the weight of the item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
i
l
in inference rule (
push
-2).

∎

4.4
Complexity Analysis

Let
\mathcal{X}_{1}
and
\mathcal{X}_{2}
be the sets of nonterminals or stack symbols of the controllers and of the controllees, respectively.
An algorithm based on one of our deductive systems would would need to store a weight for each item
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
l
j
k
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
and
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
i
j
, for all
i,j,k,l\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[0\mathord{:}n]}
,
\mathsfit A\in\mathcal{X}_{1}
, and
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in\mathcal{X}_{2}
, giving a space complexity of
\mathcal{O}(n^{4}|\mathcal{X}_{1}||\mathcal{X}_{2}|^{2})
and
\mathcal{O}(n^{2}|\mathcal{X}_{1}||\mathcal{X}_{2}|)
, respectively.
Overall we get a space complexity of
\mathcal{O}(n^{4}|\mathcal{X}_{1}|^{2}|\mathcal{X}_{2}|)
.

Computing the weight of each new item requires in the worst case inference rule (
push
-1), iterating over indices
i,j,k,l,m,n\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[0\mathord{:}n]}
, and symbols
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y},\mathit{Z}\in\mathcal{X}_{2}
and
\mathsfit A,\mathsfit B,\mathsfit C\in\mathcal{X}_{1}
.
This gives a runtime of
\mathcal{O}(n^{6}|\mathcal{X}_{1}|^{3}|\mathcal{X}_{2}|^{3})
.

4.5
Comparison with Existing Algorithms

Vijay-Shanker and Weir 1989
designed a recognition (which is essentially computing stringsums in the boolean semiring) algorithm for a type of LIG that is d-strongly equivalent to a CFG in Chomsky normal form controlled by a simple PDA.
Our stringsum algorithm is more space-efficient than theirs by a factor of
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|
and more time-efficient by a factor of
n|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}|
, where
n
is the string length,
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|
is the size of the stack alphabet, and
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}|
is the size of the alphabet of nonterminals.
Their algorithm could be improved using a trick similar to the one used by
Butoi et al. 2022
, resulting in an algorithm with the same runtime as ours.
But, in order to do so, it is necessary to store additional items, which increases the space complexity by another factor of
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|
.
Additionally, on the type of LIG used by
Vijay-Shanker and Weir 1989
, we get a further runtime improvementof a factor of
\mathcal{O}(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|)
.

For EPDA, our stringsum algorithm is more space-efficient than the algorithm of
Alonso et al. 2001
by a factor of
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|^{2}
and has an improved runtime by a factor of
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|^{3}
, where
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|
is the size of the stack alphabet. Their algorithm is designed for an EPDA without finite-state control, which is equivalent to a PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA where both the controller and the controllee are single-state. Therefore, we exclude the states when comparing the two algorithms.

5
Computing Allsums

We reuse the notion of pop computation that we defined in
section

4.1
in order to derive a space-efficient algorithm for computing allsums of CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG.
Allsums of PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG, CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA, and PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA can be computed similarly (see
appendix

D
).
We define a new type of pop computation that will help us compute the weight of
all
derivations instead of derivations of a particular string.

Definition 11
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
be a controller WCFG and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
a WLD-CFG, with nonterminal alphabets
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
, respectively.
Let be a symbol not occurring in the rules of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
.
A
pop computation
of type
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
, where
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
and
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
, is a pair of partial derivations
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})
, where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1}

\displaystyle\in({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2}

\displaystyle\in({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{2}})

where
\boldsymbol{s}_{1},\boldsymbol{s}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
and
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}^{*}}]^{*}}
.
The weight of this pop computation is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1})\otimes{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})
.

A
pop computation
of type
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
is a partial derivation
{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}}
, where
\boldsymbol{s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
.

Figure

4
b shows the deduction system for computing allsums. Just as for stringsums, we distinguish two types of items, those of type
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
and
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
.

Theorem 2
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
be a controller WCFG with nonterminal alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
and start symbol
\mathsfit S
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
a WLD-CFG with nonterminal alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
. Assume that both have weights in an
\omega
-continuous semiring (
section

A.1
).
Then the allsum of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
is
the value of the item
\mathit{S}[\mathsfit{S}]
in the deductive system of
Figure

4
b.

Unlike for stringsums, this deduction system has cycles. The value of the goal item
\mathit{S}[\mathsfit{S}]
can be found using fixed-point iteration
(
Goodman 1999
)
or the semiring generalization of Newton’s method
(
Esparza et al. 2007
)
.

Since there are various algorithms for computing item values, we don’t analyze time complexity, but only space complexity.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
be the sets of nonterminals or stack symbols of the controller and the controllee, respectively.
As the algorithm needs to store an item of the form
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
for each
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
and
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
, and an item of the form
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
for each
\mathit{X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
and
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
, it has a space complexity of
\mathcal{O}(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}||{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}|^{2})
.
If we were to compute allsums in LIG using an algorithm based on
Vijay-Shanker and Weir 1989
’s (
Vijay-Shanker and Weir 1989
) algorithm, we would have stored
\mathcal{O}(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}|^{2}|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}|^{2})
items, therefore we have a space improvement of a factor of
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}|
.
Similarly, we get a space improvement for EPDA of a factor of
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}|^{2}
over the allsum algorithm based on
Alonso et al. 2001
’s (
Alonso et al. 2001
) algorithm.

6
Conclusion

Our work has contributed several new results and algorithms for
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{L}_{2}}{}
formalisms. We introduced semiring-weighted versions of controllable CFGs and PDAs, which give rise naturally to four semiring-weighted two-level formalisms when they are controlled by semiring-weighted CFGs and PDAs.
We also introduced a WPDA normal form that is completely analogous to the Chomsky normal form for CFGs and showed that one can derive normal forms for the two-level formalisms only from the normal forms of the controller and of the controllee.
These normal forms are also normal forms for TAG, LIG, PAA, and EPDA, respectively, and the conversions only require conversions of the controller and the controllee.
Finally, we designed new stringsum and allsum algorithms for all of these formalisms, some of which are faster or more space-efficient than several existing algorithms.

Limitations

Our stringsum algorithms can be used for LIG, EPDA, spinal TAG, and spinal PAA by first converting them to a two-level formalism, converting the resulting controller and controllee grammar/automaton into the normal form, and then merging their rules into a single set of rules.
Similarly, for a general two-level formalism, the rules of the controller and the controllee would have to be extracted from the merged rules, converted into the normal form, and merged back before using the stringsum or allsum algorithm.
Although simpler, requiring only CFG and PDA conversions, these transformations add some extra complexity.
We leave direct normal form conversions for future work.

Ethics Statement

The authors foresee no ethical concerns with the research presented in this paper.

Appendix A
Additional Preliminaries

A.1
Semirings

Definition 12
.

A
monoid
is a tuple
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W},\odot,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{I}})
, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}
is a set,
\odot
is an associative binary operation, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{I}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}
, called the
identity
element, satisfies
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{I}}\odot a=a\odot{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\textbf{I}}=a
for all
a\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}
.
If
a\odot b=b\odot a
for all
a,b
, we say that the monoid is
commutative
.

Definition 13
.

A
semiring
is a tuple
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W},\oplus,\otimes,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}\right)}

such that
\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W},\oplus,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}}\right)
is a commutative monoid and
\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W},\otimes,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}\right)
is a monoid.
Additionally,
\otimes
distributes over
\oplus
, that is,
a\otimes(b\oplus c)=a\otimes b\oplus a\otimes c
and
(a\oplus b)\otimes c=a\otimes c\oplus b\otimes c
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}}
is absorbing with respect to
\otimes
, that is,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}}\otimes a=a\otimes{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}}
.
If
\otimes
is commutative, then we say that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}
is
commutative
.

The following definitions are from
Esparza et al. 2007
:

Definition 14
.

If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W},\oplus,\otimes,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}\right)}
is a semiring, the
natural order
on
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}
is the relation
\leq
, defined such that
a\leq b\Leftrightarrow\exists d\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}:a\oplus d=b
. If
\leq
is a partial order (i.e., reflexive, antisymmetric, and transitive), then we say that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}
is
naturally ordered
.

Definition 15
.

A naturally ordered semiring
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W},\oplus,\otimes,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}\right)}
is
\omega
-continuous
if it is additionally equipped with an infinite summation operator
\bigoplus
such that:

•

For any countable sequence
(a_{1},a_{2},\ldots)
, the sequence
\left(\bigoplus_{i=1}^{k}a_{i}\right)_{k\geq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}}}
has a least upper bound in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}
and is equal to
\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i}a_{i}
.

•

Multiplication distributes over infinite sums:

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i}(c\otimes a_{i})

\displaystyle=c\otimes\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i}a_{i}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i}(a_{i}\otimes c)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\bigoplus_{i}a_{i}\Bigr)\otimes c.

•

For any partition
(I_{1},I_{2},\ldots)
of
\mathbb{N}
,

\bigoplus_{j}\bigoplus_{i\in I_{j}}a_{i}=\bigoplus_{i}a_{i}.

A.2
Weighted Pushdown Automata

We use a definition of WPDA
(
Butoi et al. 2022
)
that is more general than usual and roughly a weighted version of the extended PDAs of
Aho and Ullman 1972
and the PDAs of
Lewis and Papadimitriou 1997
.

Definition 16
.

A
weighted pushdown automaton
(WPDA) over a semiring
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}
is a tuple
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}})}\right)}
, where

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
are finite sets of states, input symbols, and stack symbols, respectively,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}\times({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}\cup\{\varepsilon\})\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
is a finite set of transitions,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\rightarrow{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}
is a transition-weighting function, and

•

(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}})
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}})}
, where
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
, are called the initial and final configurations.

If
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}},\mathit{a},q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime})\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
is a transition, we write it as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}
.
If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}})=w
, we use the notation
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}/w}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}}
.

We treat strings in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
as stacks, with the first symbol being the top of the stack and the last symbol being the bottom.

A WPDA moves from one configuration to another by following transitions of the form
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}}q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}})
, which represents a move from state
p
to state
q
, while scanning the symbol
\mathit{a}
, popping the sequence
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}
from the top of the stack and pushing the sequence
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}
.
In the following definitions, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}),{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}})}\right)}
be a WPDA.

Definition 17
.

A
configuration
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
is a pair
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
, where
q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
is the current state and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
is the current contents of the stack.
We write
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{C}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}})}
for the set of all configurations of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
.

Definition 18
.

If
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\alpha})
and
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\alpha})
are configurations of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}}w,q}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}})
is a transition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
, we write
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\alpha})\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}}(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\alpha})
to denote that configuration
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}\boldsymbol{\alpha})
can be reached from
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\boldsymbol{\alpha})
in one step using transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}
.

Definition 19
.

We say that a transition
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}
is
k
-pop,
l
-push if
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}|=k
and
|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime}|=l
.
We say that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}

scans

\mathit{a}
, and if
\mathit{a}\neq\varepsilon
, we call
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}

scanning
; otherwise, we call it
non-scanning
.

The following type of WPDA is a weighted version of the PDA used by
Lang 1974
for his recognition algorithm.

Definition 20
.

A WPDA is called
simple
if each of its transitions is
k
-pop,
l
-push for some
k\leq 1
and
l\leq 1
.

The following type of WPDA is a weighted version of the PDA used by
Hopcroft et al. 2006
and others.

Definition 21
.

A WPDA is called
top-down
if all of its transitions are
1
-pop,
and the initial and final configurations are
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathit{S})
and
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon)
, respectively.

Definition 22
.

A
run
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
from
(q_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})
to
(q_{n},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n})
is a sequence of steps
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}=(q_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{1}}\cdots\xRightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{n}}(q_{n},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n})
.
We write
(q_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})\xRightarrow{*}(q_{n},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n})
to assert that a run from
(p,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}})
to
(q,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}^{\prime})
exists, or to denote the set of all such runs.
If
(q_{0},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{0})=(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}})
and
(q_{n},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{n})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}})}
, we call
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}

accepting
. If
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{i}
scans
\mathit{a}_{i}
, for
i\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}[1\mathord{:}n]}
, then we say that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}
scans
\boldsymbol{s}=\mathit{a}_{1}\cdots\mathit{a}_{n}
.

The
weight
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}
is the product of its transition weights,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{def}}}{{=}}}\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\tau}_{i}).

We denote by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}},\boldsymbol{s})
the set all accepting runs of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
scanning
\boldsymbol{s}
, and by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}})
the set of all accepting runs of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
.

Definition 23
.

The
stringsum

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}},\boldsymbol{s})
of a string
\boldsymbol{s}
under
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
is the total weight of all accepting runs scanning
\boldsymbol{s}
,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}},\boldsymbol{s})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{def}}}{{=}}}\bigoplus_{\mathclap{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}},\boldsymbol{s})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}).

Definition 24
.

The
allsum

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}})
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
is the total weight of all its accepting runs,

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}})\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\textnormal{def}}}{{=}}}\bigoplus_{\mathclap{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Pi}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}})}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}).

Appendix B
Two-Level Formal Systems

B.1
Definitions

Having defined both WPDA (
16
) and WLD-CFG (
7
), we can give a definition for weighted
\text{PDA}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}\text{CFG}
.

Definition 25
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
be a controller WPDA with states
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
, stack alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}
and initial configuration
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}})
,
and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
be a controllee WLD-CFG with nonterminals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
. Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
is a rewriting system with rules as follows.

(a)

If
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}})
is a transition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
, then

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
has a rule for each
\mathit{X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}[q,\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{w}\mathit{X}[r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}.

(b)

If
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{\ell/w_{1}}s,\varepsilon})
is a transition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
, and
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\colon\mathit{X}\xrightarrow{w_{2}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{317782}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{194859}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}})
is a production of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
has a rule

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}[r,\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}]\,\mathit{Y}[s,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}]}.

Next, we define WLD-PDAs by analogy with WLD-CFGs. This definition is a weighted version of our previous definition
(
Butoi et al. 2023
)
.

Definition 26
.

A
weighted labeled distinguished pushdown automaton
(WLD-PDA) over a semiring
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{W}}={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\left({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W},\oplus,\otimes,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\mathbf{0}}},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}\right)}
is a tuple
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}},(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathit{S}),(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon))
, where

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}
,
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}
are finite sets of states, input symbols, stack symbols and labels, respectively,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}L}\times\mathbb{N}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}\times({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}\cup\{\varepsilon\})\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}
is a finite set of transitions,

•

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathrm{w}}\colon{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}\to{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}W}
is the transition-weighting function, and

•

(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathit{S})
and
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon)
, where
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q},\mathit{S}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
, are the initial and final configurations.

If
(\ell,i,q,\mathsfit A,\mathit{a},r,\mathsfit B_{1}\cdots\mathsfit B_{n})
is a transition in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}
, we must have either
i=0
, which we write as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}}r,\mathsfit B_{1}\cdots\mathsfit B_{n}}
, or
1\leq i\leq n
, which we write as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}}r,\mathsfit B_{1}\cdots\mathsfit B_{i-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{598534}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{i}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{598534}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{i}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{500741}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{i}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{450171}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit B_{i}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\mathsfit B_{i+1}\cdots\mathsfit B_{n}}
.

A WLD-PDA behaves similarly to a WPDA, but its runs are controlled by either a controller CFG or PDA.
As in
section

3
, the rules of the controller and of the controllee can be merged into a single system.

Definition 27
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
be a controller CFG with nonterminals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
and start symbol
\mathsfit S
, and
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
be a WLD-PDA with stack alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
and states
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
is a rewriting system with rules as follows.

(a)

If
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{w}\boldsymbol{\beta}})
is a production of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
, where
\boldsymbol{\beta}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}^{*}}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
has a transition for each
\mathit{X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
and
q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w}q,\mathit{X}[\boldsymbol{\beta}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}.

(b)

If
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{w_{1}}\ell})
is a production of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\colon q,\mathit{X}\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}/w_{2}}r,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{317782}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{194859}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}}
is a transition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
has a transition

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}/w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}r,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}[\mathsfit S]\,\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}[\mathsfit S]}.

Definition 28
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
be a controller PDA with stack alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}
and start configuration
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}})
, and
let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
be a WLD-PDA with stack alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{2}
and states
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}_{2}
.
Then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
is a rewriting system with rules as follows.

(a)

If
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}})
is a transition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
has a transition for each
\mathit{X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
and
p\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}_{2}
:

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,\mathit{X}[q,\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w}p,\mathit{X}[r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}.

(b)

If
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}r,\mathsfit A\xrightarrow{\ell/w_{1}}s,\varepsilon})
is a transition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\colon p,\mathit{X}\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}/w_{2}}q,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{317782}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{194859}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}}
is a transition of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
, then
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
has a transition

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,\mathit{X}[r,\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\xrightarrow{\mathit{a}/w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}q,\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{1}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}]\mathit{Y}[s,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\boldsymbol{\alpha}_{2}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}]}.

The terms scanning/non-scanning transitions and (accepting) runs are defined analogously to those for WPDAs.

B.2
Normal Forms

B.2.1
WCFG normal form

Proposition
1
.

For any WCFG
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
with weights in an
\omega
-continuous semiring, there is a WCFG in Chomsky normal form that defines the same weighted language as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{1}
.

For any WLD-CFG
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
with weights in an
\omega
-continuous semiring, there is a WLD-CFG in Chomsky normal form that is equivalent to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}
.

Proof.

The proof for WCFGs is a generalization of the standard conversion for unweighted grammars
(
Hopcroft et al. 2006
)
, and is related to the Earley-style algorithm of
Stolcke 1995
.

1.

If
S
is the old start symbol, add a new nonterminal
S^{\prime}
, a production
S^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}S
, and make
S^{\prime}
the new start symbol.

2.

For every terminal
a
, create a production
T_{a}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}a
, where
T_{a}
is a fresh nonterminal, and replace every right-hand side occurrence of
a
with
T_{a}
.

3.

Replace every production
X\xrightarrow{w}Y_{1}\cdots Y_{k}
, where
k>2
, with the productions

\displaystyle X

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{w}Y_{1}Z_{1}

\displaystyle Z_{1}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}Y_{2}Z_{2}

\displaystyle\mathmakebox[\widthof{{}\rightarrow{}}][c]{\vdots}

\displaystyle Z_{k-2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}Y_{k-1}Y_{k}

where
Z_{1},\ldots,Z_{k-2}
are fresh nonterminals.

4.

For each nonterminal
X
, compute the weight of all partial derivations
X\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon
using the deductive system

\begin{array}[]{cr@{}l}\frac{\normalsize}{\normalsize A\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon}&A&{}\xrightarrow{w}\varepsilon\\
\frac{\normalsize B\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon}{\normalsize A\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon}&A&{}\xrightarrow{w}B\\
\frac{\normalsize B\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon\quad C\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon}{\normalsize A\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon}&A&{}\xrightarrow{w}BC\end{array}

Then for every production
X\xrightarrow{w}YZ
with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}(Y\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon)=w_{Y}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}(Z\xRightarrow{*}\varepsilon)=w_{Z}
, add productions
X\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{Y}}Z
and
X\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{Z}}Y
. Finally, for all
X
, remove all productions
X\rightarrow\varepsilon
.

5.

For all nonterminals
X
and
Y
, compute the weight of all partial derivations
X\xRightarrow{*}Y
using the deductive system

\begin{array}[]{cr@{}l}\frac{\normalsize}{\normalsize A\xRightarrow{*}A}\\
\frac{\normalsize A\xRightarrow{*}B}{\normalsize A\xRightarrow{*}C}&B&\xrightarrow{w}C\\
\end{array}

Then for every production
X\xrightarrow{w_{2}}YZ
and nonterminal
W\neq X
, with
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}(W\xRightarrow{*}X)=w_{1}
, add production
W\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}YZ
. Finally, for all
X
,
Y
, remove all productions
X\rightarrow Y
.

Now we give a similar construction for WLD-CFGs.
Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
be a WLD-CFG and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{F}}
a controller WCFG or WPDA, both with weights from an
\omega
-continuous semiring.
Then there exists a WLD-CFG in normal form
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}^{\prime}
and a controller
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}
such that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{F}}^{\prime}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}^{\prime}
defines the same weighted language as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{F}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
.
We assume for simplicity that
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{F}}
is a controller WCFG; a similar construction can be given when it is a WPDA.

Converting a
\text{CFG}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}\text{CFG}
to normal form requires an allsum algorithm that works on any binarized
\text{CFG}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}\text{CFG}
, not necessarily in normal form.
We must use the rules shown in
fig.

5
in addition to those from
fig.

4
b when computing allsums.

Figure 5:
Additional deduction rules for computing allsums in binarized CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}

Root–foot transform

The nullary and unary removal steps below are analogous to nullary and unary removal in a WCFG. However, sometimes, when we remove nullary/unary rules, we need information from both the controller and controllee to compute their lost weight. To facilitate this, we define the following transformation, which copies information from the controllee to the controller.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
be the controller and controllee nonterminal alphabet, respectively.

1.

For every controllee rule
\ell:\mathit{X}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\alpha}
where
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
has no distinguished symbol, relabel the rule as
{\mathsfit\ell}^{\mathit{X}}_{\bot}
.

2.

For every controllee rule
\ell:\mathit{X}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{\alpha}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{317782}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{194859}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\boldsymbol{\beta}
, relabel the rule as
{\mathsfit\ell}^{\mathit{X}}_{\mathit{Y}}
.

3.

Perform the construction of
Bar-Hillel et al. 1961
on the controller, using controllee nonterminals instead of states. That is, relabel every controller nonterminal
\mathsfit A
as
{\mathsfit A}^{\mathit{X}}_{\bot}
or
{\mathsfit A}^{\mathit{X}}_{\mathit{Y}}
for all
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
, making as many copies of rules as needed, subject to the constraints that (i) if the lhs is
{\mathsfit A}^{\mathit{X}}_{\mathit{Y}}
(where
Y
could be
\bot
) then the first rhs symbol must be
{\mathsfit A}^{\mathit{X}}_{\mathit{Y}^{\prime}}
and the last rhs symbol must be
{\mathsfit A}^{\mathit{X}^{\prime}}_{\mathit{Y}}
; (ii) if
{\mathsfit A}^{\mathit{X}}_{\mathit{Y}}
is an rhs nonterminal then its successor (if any) must be
{\mathsfit B}^{\mathit{Y}}_{\mathit{Z}}
.

4.

Add a new controller start symbol
\mathsfit S^{\prime}
with rules
\mathsfit S^{\prime}\rightarrow{\mathsfit S}^{\mathit{X}}_{\bot}
for all
\mathit{X}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
.

Controllee binarization

In the controllee, replace every production
\ell:\mathit{X}\xrightarrow{w}\mathit{Y}_{1}\cdots{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{634189}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{634189}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{537906}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{414983}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\cdots\mathit{Y}_{k}
, where
k>2
, with the productions

\displaystyle\ell_{1}:\mathit{X}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{w}\mathit{Y}_{1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{556765}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{556765}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{469099}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365402}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}

\displaystyle\ell_{2}:\mathit{Z}_{1}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}\mathit{Y}_{2}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{556765}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{556765}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{469099}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{365402}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}

\displaystyle\mathmakebox[\widthof{{}\rightarrow{}}][c]{\vdots}

\displaystyle\ell_{d-1}:\mathit{Z}_{d-2}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}\mathit{Y}_{d-1}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{566031}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{566031}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{484601}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{380904}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}

\displaystyle\ell_{d}:\mathit{Z}_{d}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{1096877}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{1096877}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{932440}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{828743}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+1}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\mathit{Y}_{k}

\displaystyle\ell_{d+1}:\mathit{Z}_{d+1}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{1096877}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{1096877}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{932440}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{828743}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}_{d+2}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\mathit{Y}_{k-1}

\displaystyle\mathmakebox[\widthof{{}\rightarrow{}}][c]{\vdots}

\displaystyle\ell_{k-1}:\mathit{Z}_{k-1}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{634189}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{634189}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{537906}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{414983}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}_{d}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\mathit{Y}_{d+1}

where
\mathit{Z}_{1},\ldots,\mathit{Z}_{k-2}
are fresh controllee nonterminals.

Accordingly, in the controller, replace every rhs occurrence of
\ell
with
\ell_{1}\cdots\ell_{k-1}
.

Controllee nullary removal

Like the standard nullary removal algorithm
(
Hopcroft et al. 2006
)
, nullary removal in an WLD-CFG has three steps: partitioning, precomputation, and removal. However, removing nullary rules from the controllee also sometimes requires modifications to the controller.

1.

Partition: Replace every controllee nonterminal
\mathit{X}
with two nonterminals
\mathit{X}_{\varepsilon}
and
\mathit{X}_{\mathrlap{\!\not}\varepsilon}
.
Every rule with
k
rhs nonterminals becomes
2^{k}
rules with all possible combination of rhs nonterminals, and the lhs annotated
\varepsilon
iff the rhs is empty or all the rhs symbols are annotated
\varepsilon
. The start symbol is
\mathit{S}_{\mathrlap{\!\not}\varepsilon}
.

2.

Precompute: Compute the allsum
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}\left({{\hbox to22.78pt{\vbox to20.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 11.38823pt\lower-3.04544pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{
{}{{}}{}{{{}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \color[rgb]{1,1,1}{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 -3.94 M 0 -3.94 C 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 C 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 C 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 C 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 0 -3.94 Z M 0 -3.94}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{{}}{}{{}}{}{{}}
{}{}{{}}
{}{}{{}}
{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} {}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 11.81 L -11.81 0 L 11.81 0 L 0 11.81 Z}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
\lxSVG@closescope {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-9.55508pt}{11.94449pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
}
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}}\right)
for all
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{1},\mathit{X}_{\varepsilon}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}_{2}
.

3.

Remove (non-distinguished): For every non-distinguished occurrence of
\mathit{X}_{\varepsilon}
in the rhs of a controllee rule
\pi
, delete the occurrence and multiply the weight of
\pi
by
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}\left({{\hbox to22.07pt{\vbox to20.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 11.033pt\lower-3.04544pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}

Remove (distinguished): The distinguished occurrences of
\mathit{X}_{\varepsilon}
cannot be removed in the same way, because we don’t know what weight to multiply in. Instead, perform the root–foot transform. Then, for every occurrence of

on the rhs of a controller rule
\pi

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}\left({{\hbox to22.78pt{\vbox to20.95pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 11.38823pt\lower-3.04544pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{

\lxSVG@closescope {}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{}}{}{}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

B.2.2
WPDA normal form

We first define a WPDA normal form that is analogous to Chomsky normal form for WCFGs.

Definition 29
.

A WPDA is in
normal form
if all of its transitions are of one of the following types: (1)
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathit{S}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon}
, (2) scanning and
0
-push, or (3) non-scanning and
2
-push. Moreover,
\mathit{S}
is not pushed by any transition.

A WLD-PDA is in normal form if all of its transitions are of type (1) or (2) above, (3a)
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathit{X}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w}r,{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{317782}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{194859}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\mathit{Z}}
, or (3b)
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q,\mathit{X}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w}r,\mathit{Y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{321562}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{321562}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{264477}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.81778pt$\hss\resizebox{160780}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}}
.

Proposition 2
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
be a WPDA with weights from an
\omega
-continuous semiring. Then there is a WPDA in normal form that defines the same weighted language as
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
.

For any WLD-PDA
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
with weights in an
\omega
-continuous semiring, there is a WLD-PDA in Chomsky normal form that is equivalent to
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
.

Proof.

In previous work
(
Butoi et al. 2022
)
, we gave a conversion from arbitrary WPDAs to a normal form that is close to
29
, but allows transitions that are scanning but are 1-push or 2-push. We can modify the conversion using the following preparatory steps:

1.

If
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}})
is the old start configuration, add a new state
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}^{\prime}
, a new stack symbol
S^{\prime}
, and a transition
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}^{\prime},S^{\prime}\xrightarrow{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\gamma}}_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}
, and make
(q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}^{\prime},S^{\prime})
the new start configuration.

2.

For every terminal
a
, add a fresh stack symbol
T_{a}
. Replace every scanning transition
(q,X\xrightarrow{a/w}r,\gamma)
with
(q,X\xrightarrow{\epsilon/w}r,T_{a}\gamma)
, and add transitions
(q,T_{a}\xrightarrow{a/{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{{\mathbf{1}}}}}q,\varepsilon)
for every state
q
.

3.

Remove all nullary transitions
(
Butoi et al. 2022
, §3.2)
.

4.

Remove all unary transitions
(
Butoi et al. 2022
, §3.3)
.

A WLD-PDA can always be converted into an equivalent WLD-CFG
Butoi et al. 2023
, converted into the normal form, then converted back into into a WLD-PDA.
∎

To convert a WLD-PDA to normal form, we use the construction above, modified by analogy with
proposition

1
.
In the root–foot transform, each nonterminal includes two or four states (just as allsum items do).
The nullary removal procedure for WPDAs is more complex than for WCFGs, but the required modification is analogous: we remove non-distinguished stack symbols
\mathit{X}_{\varepsilon}
as usual, but when removing a distinguished stack symbol
\mathit{X}_{\varepsilon}
, we must multiply its weight in the controller rather than the controllee.

\begin{array}[]{r@{}lr@{}lr@{}lc}\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}\hfil\lx@intercol&\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}\hfil\lx@intercol&\lx@intercol\hfil{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}_{2}\hfil\lx@intercol&\text{name}\\
\hline\cr{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathsfit S}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{\ell/w_{1}}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\colon\mathit{S}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{2}}\varepsilon}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{S}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathsfit S]}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}\varepsilon}&\text{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}epsilon})}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,\mathsfit A}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{\ell/w_{1}}q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f},\varepsilon}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\colon\mathit{X}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{2}}\mathit{a}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit A]}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}\mathit{a}}&\text{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}terminal})}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,\mathsfit A}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{\ell/w_{1}}q,\varepsilon}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\colon\mathit{X}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{2}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{389719}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{317782}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{194859}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Y}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}\mathit{Z}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}\mathit{Y}[q,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]\mathit{Z}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathsfit S]}&\text{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}left pop})}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,\mathsfit A}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{\ell/w_{1}}q,\varepsilon}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\ell\colon\mathit{X}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{2}}\mathit{Y}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathchoice{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{321562}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{321562}{254863}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\textstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{264477}{204801}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}{\mathop{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern 0.80002pt$\hss\resizebox{160780}{177495}{\rotatebox[origin]{90.0}{(}}\hss$\crcr\cr$\hss\scriptscriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{Z}}\hss$\crcr}}}\limits}}}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{1}\otimes w_{2}}\mathit{Y}[q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota},\mathsfit S]\mathit{Z}[q,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}&\text{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}right pop})}\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}p,\mathsfit A}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon/w_{1}}q,\mathsfit B\mathsfit C}&&&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathit{X}[q,\mathsfit A\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}&{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{}\xrightarrow{w_{1}}\mathit{X}[q,\mathsfit B\mathsfit C\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]}&\text{({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}push})}\end{array}

\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}

B.2.3
Two-level normal forms

Figure

6
shows how the normal forms of PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG, CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA and PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA are obtained from the normal forms of the controllees and the controllers.
For the
epsilon
rules, as noted in
section

3.2
, in principle there could be rules with other left-hand sides, but such rules would never be used.

Definition 30
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
be a controller WPDA with states
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
, and stack alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
be a WLD-CFG with nonterminals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
.
Let
\boldsymbol{s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
be a string of length
|\boldsymbol{s}|=n
.
A
pop computation
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
of type
\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
i
l
j
k
\mathit{Y}[q,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
, where
0\leq i\leq j\leq k\leq l\leq n
,
p,q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
,
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
, and
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
, is a pair of partial derivations
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})
, where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1}

\displaystyle\in({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[q,\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2}

\displaystyle\in({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{(k\mathord{:}l]}}).

A
pop computation
of type
\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit{A}]
i
j
is a partial derivation

.

Definition 31
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
be a controller WCFG with nonterminals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}

\boldsymbol{s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}

|\boldsymbol{s}|=n
.

pop computation
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}

i,p
l,s
j,q
k,r
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]

,

\displaystyle\in(({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}p},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]})\xRightarrow{*}({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}q},{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}))\text{\ and scans {\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531} $\boldsymbol{s}_{(i\mathord{:}j]}$}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2}

\displaystyle\in(({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}r},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}})\xRightarrow{*}({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}s},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\varepsilon}))\text{\ and scans {\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531} $\boldsymbol{s}_{(k\mathord{:}l]}$}}.

pop computation
of type

i,p
j,q

scanning

.

C.2
Deduction Systems

Figures

8
,
7
and
9
, in the columns labeled “stringsum,” show deduction systems for computing stringsums of PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG, CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA, and PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA. The controller and controllee PDAs are assumed to be single-state.

The goal items for PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
CFG, CFG
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA, and PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA are, in order,
\mathit{S}[q_{\iota},\mathsfit{S}]
0
n
,
\mathit{S}[\mathsfit{S}]
0,q_{\iota}
n,q_{f}
, and
\mathit{S}[q_{\iota}^{1},\mathsfit{S}]
0,q_{\iota}^{2}
n,q_{f}
, where
n
is the length of the input string.

Deductive systems for computing stringsums and allsums of PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}
PDA. States

and
q_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\iota}}^{2}
are the initial states of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
, respectively. State
q_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}f}
is the final state of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}

Appendix D
Computing Allsums

Definition 33
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
be a controller WPDA with states
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
and stack alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
be a WLD-CFG with nonterminal alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
.
A
pop computation
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
of type
\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
\mathit{Y}[q,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
, where
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
,
p,q\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
, and
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
, is a pair of partial derivations
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})
, where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1}

\displaystyle\in({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit A]}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{1}}{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[q,\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}})

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2}

\displaystyle\in({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}\xRightarrow{*}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}_{2}})

and
\boldsymbol{s}_{1},\boldsymbol{s}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
,
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}^{*}}]^{*}}
.
The weight of this pop computation is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{1})\otimes{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}d}_{2})
.

A
pop computation
of type
\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit{A}]
is a partial derivation
\mathit{X}[p,\mathsfit A]\xRightarrow{*}\boldsymbol{s}
, where
\boldsymbol{s}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
.

Definition 34
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}
be a controller CFG with nonterminal alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
, and let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
be a WLD-PDA with states
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
and stack alphabet
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
.
A
pop computation
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{G}}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}
of type
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}\,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
p
s
q
r
\mathit{Y}[\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
, where
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
,
p,q,r,s\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
, and
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
, is a pair of runs
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2})
, where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1}

\displaystyle\in(({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}p},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]})\xRightarrow{*}({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}q},{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}))\text{\ and scans\ {\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531} $\boldsymbol{s}_{1}$}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2}

\displaystyle\in(({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}r},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}})\xRightarrow{*}({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}s},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\varepsilon}))\text{\ and scans\ {\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531} $\boldsymbol{s}_{2}$}}

and
\boldsymbol{s}_{1},\boldsymbol{s}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
,
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}^{*}}]^{*}}
.
The weight of this pop computation is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1})\otimes{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2})
.

A
pop computation
of type
\mathit{X}[\mathsfit{A}]
p
q
is a run
({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}p},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[\mathsfit A]})\xRightarrow{*}({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}q},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\varepsilon})
scanning some
{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
.

Definition 35
.

Let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
be a controller WPDA and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
a WLD-PDA.
Additionally, let
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}
, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}_{2}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{2}
be the states and stack alphabets of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
, respectively.
A
pop computation
of
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{1}\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{P}}_{2}
of type

s

r
\mathit{Y}[f,\mathinner{\cdot\cdot}]
, where
p,q,r,s\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}_{2}
,
e,f\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}_{1}
,
\mathit{X},\mathit{Y}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{2}
and
\mathsfit A\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}
, is is a pair of runs
({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2})
, where

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1}

\displaystyle\in(({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}p},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathit{X}[e,\mathsfit A]})\xRightarrow{*}({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}q},{\color[rgb]{0,0.4688,0.582}\mathit{Y}[f,\varepsilon]}{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}}))\text{\ and scans\ {\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531} $\boldsymbol{s}_{1}$}}

\displaystyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2}

\displaystyle\in(({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}r},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}})\xRightarrow{*}({\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}s},{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\varepsilon}))\text{\ and scans\ {\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531} $\boldsymbol{s}_{2}$}}

and
\boldsymbol{s}_{1},\boldsymbol{s}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
,
\mathbf{\Upsilon}_{2}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{2}[{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}_{1}^{*}}]^{*}}
.
The weight of this pop computation is
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1},{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2})={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{1})\otimes{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathbf{w}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\boldsymbol{\pi}}_{2})
.

of type

scanning some
{\color[rgb]{0.6406,0.0273,0.4531}\boldsymbol{s}}\in{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Sigma}^{*}}
.

Figures

8
,

and

, in the columns labeled “allsum”, show deductive systems for computing allsums of PDA
\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}

\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}

\mathrel{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\triangleright}}

As in
section

5
, the value of the goal item can be computed by fixed-point iteration or the semiring generalization of Newton’s method.

Appendix E
Complexity Analysis of Conversions

Our stringsum and allsum algorithms, although designed for the two-level formalisms, can also be used for TAG, LIG, PAA, and EPDA.
However, we must apply a series of conversions in order to do this.
For instance, we must apply the following transformations to a WLIG with sets of nonterminals
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
and stack symbols
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
before feeding it into the stringsum algorithm:

1.

The controller WPDA and the controllee WCFG have to be extracted from the WLIG, resulting in a WCFG with
\mathcal{O}(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}|)
nonterminals and a WPDA with
\mathcal{O}(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|)
stack symbols.

2.

The controllee WCFG must be converted into Chomsky normal form.
If the productions have at most
k
symbols on the right-hand side, then the output WCFG has
\mathcal{O}(k\times|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{R}}|)
nonterminals.
Similarly, the controller WPDA must be converted into the normal form.
If the WPDA has transitions that push at most
k
symbols, the resulting WPDA has
\mathcal{O}(k\times|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\delta}|)
states.

3.

The rules of the controller and of the controllee need to be merged back into a set of WLIG rules. This construction outputs a WLIG with
\mathcal{O}(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}|\times|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}|)
nonterminals and
\mathcal{O}(|{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}|)
stack symbols, where
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{N}}
is the set of nonterminals of the input controllee, and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}Q}
and
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\Gamma}
are the sets of states and stack symbols of the input controller.
